+ " BPM, your average resting heart rate shows your heart is working as it should when relaxed. Continuing with your current habits will help keep things aligned."
+ " BPM, your average resting heart rate shows your heart working overtime when relaxed. Deep breathing and daily cardio can help ease its workload."
+ " Needs Attention"
+ " expandedFrom "
+ " shows your body is missing out on the deep rest it needs to repair itself. Improving your habits can help you for the years ahead."
+ " shows your body working harder to use oxygen when active. More activity that gets your heart going can help better support how your body ages over time."
+ " shows your heart and lungs are highly efficient. This helps make everyday movement easier and protects your body long term."
+ " shows you’re getting highly restorative rest. This deep, consistent sleep is a great way to protect your brain and body long-term."
+ " shows you’re getting the restorative rest expected for your actual age. Sticking to your current habits will help your body continue to age on track."

+ "+1.1 to +5.9 Years"
+ ", your Hemoglobin A1c shows your body is highly effective at managing sugar. Keeping your levels steady is a great way to protect your energy as you age."
+ ", your Hemoglobin A1c shows your body is managing energy exactly as it should. Maintaining your current habits will help your body continue to age as expected."
+ ", your Hemoglobin A1c shows your body is using significant effort to balance your sugar. Working on habits that keep your levels steady is key to aging well."
+ ", your Hemoglobin A1c shows your body is working harder to balance your energy. Staying mindful of your daily habits can help protect your system over time."
+ ", your Hemoglobin A1c shows your body processes energy well, helping your system run smoothly long term."

+ "Hemoglobin A1c Is on Hold for Health Age"
+ "Hemoglobin A1c and Aging"
+ "Hemoglobin A1c provides a helpful snapshot of your average glucose, or blood sugar, over the last few months. Staying in a normal range means your body is doing a great job processing energy, which is vital for aging well.\n\nBecause A1c reflects months of data, this number shifts gradually, but steady changes now can lead to meaningful progress. Focusing on a balanced diet, staying active, and managing your weight are some of the best ways to support your metabolic health. If your results are running high, a conversation with your doctor is recommended."
+ "Here’s how your hearing is doing based on your data."
+ "Here’s how your heart is doing based on your data."
+ "Here’s how your mental wellbeing is doing based on your data."
+ "Here’s how your metabolic health is doing based on your data."
+ "Here’s how your movement is doing based on your data."
+ "Here’s how your nutrition is doing based on your data."
+ "Here’s how your sleep is doing based on your data."
+ "Here’s how you’re doing based on your data."
+ "How Health Age Works"
+ "Jade Wu is a board-certified behavioral sleep medicine specialist. She earned her PhD in clinical psychology from Boston University, completed her training at Duke University School of Medicine, and is licensed only in the United States."
+ "LoweringYourHealthAge"
+ "Mike Natter is a board-certified internist and endocrinologist. He earned his medical degree from Sidney Kimmel Medical College at Thomas Jefferson University, completed his training at NYU and Bellevue Hospital, and is licensed only in the United States."
+ "More Data Needed for Hemoglobin A1c"
+ "More on Hemoglobin A1c"
+ "Niro Feliciano is a certified cognitive behavioral therapist. She earned her master of science in social work from Columbia University and is licensed only in the United States."
+ "Often called “bad” cholesterol, LDL can build up in your arteries over time and age your heart prematurely. Keeping levels in check through diet, exercise, or medication is one of the most effective ways to protect your long-term health. LDL tends to move gradually, so steady changes over time make the biggest difference."

+ "Section header above the educational content on the Hemoglobin A1c Health Age metric detail page."
+ "Signed age-delta label rendered as the primary value on the Health Age channel in the Longevity tab, when the delta rounds to exactly one year. Channel subtitles are title case. Argument is the delta, either +1 or -1."
+ "Signed age-delta label rendered as the primary value on the Health Age channel in the Longevity tab. Channel subtitles are title case. Argument is the delta in years: whole when it rounds to a whole year (a reading that rounds away always draws \"-0\", and so do the ends of the algorithm's range), a signed tenth otherwise."
+ "Subtitle on the Overview channel in the Longevity tab. Channel subtitles are title case. Argument is the number of health categories classified as Excellent."
+ "Subtitle on the Overview channel in the Longevity tab. Channel subtitles are title case. Argument is the number of health categories classified as Fair."
+ "Subtitle on the Overview channel in the Longevity tab. Channel subtitles are title case. Argument is the number of health categories classified as Good."
+ "Subtitle on the Overview channel in the Longevity tab. Channel subtitles are title case. Argument is the number of health categories classified as Needs Attention."
+ "Subtitle under a metric value that is no longer current. The argument is the date or date range the value covers, e.g. 'Sept 23' or 'May 4 – Jul 2'."

- "Heart Rate Variability"
- "Hemoglobin A1c provides a helpful snapshot of your average glucose, or blood sugar, over the last few months. Staying in the normal range—ideally 5.6% or lower—means your body is doing a great job processing energy, which is vital for aging well.\n\nBecause A1c reflects months of data, this number shifts gradually, but steady changes now can lead to meaningful progress. Focusing on a balanced diet, staying active, and managing your weight are some of the best ways to support your metabolic health. If your results are running high, a conversation with your doctor is recommended."

- "Often called “bad” cholesterol, LDL can build up in your arteries over time and age your heart prematurely. Keeping levels below 100 through diet, exercise, or medication is one of the most effective ways to protect your long-term health. LDL tends to move gradually, so steady changes over time make the biggest difference."
